_source line 9810 · 2026-05-20T02:26:35.356420+00:00_

1	\section{Limitations}\label{sec:limitations}
2	
3	\paragraph{Single dataset.}
4	All experiments use one dataset (HarmThoughts~\citep{harmthoughts}), which provides step-level CP annotations derived from manual inspection of reasoning chains. The dataset spans multiple harmful categories (e.g., violence, fraud, illegal activities) but we do not stratify results by category; categories with longer average reasoning chains or earlier CPs may exhibit different probe dynamics. CP positions range from early to late within traces, though the distribution is right-skewed (most CPs occur in the first half of reasoning). Annotation granularity (step-level vs.\ token-level), category distribution, and annotator team composition may all influence which layers carry discriminative signal and how strongly metric bias manifests. Inter-annotator agreement (IAA) was not computed for these annotations. Random annotation noise would attenuate effect sizes symmetrically, making our estimates conservative; systematic noise (e.g., consistent early or late CP placement) could bias layer selection or inflate modality differences. Without IAA, we cannot distinguish these cases, so our conclusions may not transfer to independently annotated corpora. Generalization to other jailbreak datasets, attack taxonomies, or multilingual settings is untested.
5	
6	\paragraph{Model-specific caveats and statistical power.}
7	Current probes characterize the commitment phenomenon rather than constitute production detectors: false positive rates remain 11--50\% (model-dependent), and the 76.7\% oracle gap (R1-8B; \cref{sec:oracle}) underscores how much room remains. R1-32B achieves significance only at $W{=}1$ (25\% coverage, 5/20 traces) and its HS advantage shows a pattern consistent with position mediation (residualized gap +1.9pp, $p{=}0.914$; \cref{app:position_residual}). ### Tool result

_source line 9844 · 2026-05-20T03:59:49.887998+00:00_

1	Reasoning models with chain-of-thought (CoT) can self-jailbreak: their reasoning traces transition from safe deliberation to committed harmful generation without adversarial input. Detecting the commitment point (CP)---the first reasoning step after which safety deliberation permanently ceases---requires monitoring internal representations or generated text. We study four reasoning LLMs, primarily Qwen-based (3/4 models) with one Llama model for limited cross-architecture evidence, on HarmThoughts and provide initial characterization of hidden-state (HS) vs.\ text probe capabilities on this benchmark. The comparison depends critically on which transformer layer is monitored: a crossing-rate composite metric masks the HS advantage at 32B scale by selecting shallow layers where representation compression inflates threshold crossings. Threshold-based layer selection recovers HS precision gains of +15.4 to +21.9pp against MiniLM-384d (Holm-Bonferroni corrected; advantage is encoder-conditional and represents an upper bound); this advantage diminishes with stronger encoders (384d: 4/4 models pass HB $\to$ 1024d BGE-large: 1/4). The 7--8B results are cross-validated (nested CV): precision and balanced accuracy confirmed; R1-8B safe-trace false positive rate (FPR) is significant but seed-sensitive (3/5 seeds pass HB). The 32B results are exploratory$^\dagger$ ($^\dagger$\,sample-limited, $n \leq 20$): QwQ-32B is significant but suggestive of position-competitiveness; R1-32B shows a pattern consistent with position mediation and falls below minimum detectable effect. A three-layer ensemble provides metric-agnostic HS advantage matching the best individual metric (4/4 models, $p \leq 0.002$). Metric-induced layer selection bias is the robust methodological finding; the empirical HS advantage is conditional on encoder quality, model scale, and position control.
2

1	\section{Introduction}\label{sec:introduction}
2	
3	Reasoning models such as DeepSeek-R1~\cite{deepseekr1} and QwQ~\cite{qwq} scale inference-time computation through extended chains of thought (CoT). Recent work documents that these models can ``self-jailbreak'': without adversarial input modification, the CoT transitions from safety-aware deliberation to committed harmful generation~\cite{harmthoughts,selfjailbreak}. We define the first reasoning step after which safety deliberation permanently ceases as the \textbf{Safety Commitment Point} (CP). This failure mode differs qualitatively from adversarial jailbreaks on standard LLMs because the harmful transition originates within the model's own reasoning. Before retrospective characterization can inform future detection systems, a prior question must be answered: can current probing methods reliably distinguish pre-CP from post-CP reasoning steps, and under what conditions? This paper provides retrospective classification (not online detection) of what hidden-state and text-based probes can and cannot detect; false positive rates remain 11--50\%, so deployment applications are future work.\looseness=-1
4	
5	Existing safety detectors target fundamentally different problems. Hidden-state (HS) methods such as TrajGuard~\cite{trajguard} and SafeSwitch~\cite{safeswitch} classify adversarial inputs at the token level, while text monitors like SafeRemind~\cite{saferemind} scan generated reasoning for explicit violations. Neither tracks the gradual, multi-step commitment process. Before asking which modality is better, a prior methodological question must be answered: how sensitive are probe conclusions to the choice of layer selection metric? HS probes require choosing which transformer layer to monitor, a researcher degree of freedom whose metric sensitivity has received almost no scrutiny. We show this choice alone can reverse a study's central conclusion---the same model and data yield a 14-layer (R1-8B) to 61-layer 
…[truncated 3061 chars]

1	\section{Discussion}\label{sec:discussion}
2	
3	\paragraph{Layer selection as researcher degree of freedom~\citep{gelman2013garden}.}
4	Our five-metric ablation (\cref{sec:layer-results}) shows crossing rate reverses the central finding via shallow-layer compression (\cref{app:cosine_volatility}), architecture-independent across Llama/Qwen (\cref{tab:r18b_full_layer}). A random-label control on QwQ-32B reveals crossing rate retains its shallow preference in 100\% of random-label permutations (CR-composite: 80\%), while precision's deep preference dissolves (only 20\% shallow, scattered across layers; \cref{app:random_cp}): the crossing-rate shallow preference is geometric (persisting under random labels), while threshold-based deep selection requires genuine safety labels. Contribution~1 (layer bias) thus generalizes from geometry, cross-dataset (AdvBench volatility ratio $1.67\times$; \cref{app:cosine_volatility}), and is internally valid (split-half replication; \cref{app:split_half}); Contribution~2 (HS advantage) is sample-dependent. Threshold-based layers peak at L63/64~\citep{wollschlager2025geometry}; R1-32B selectivity is 3.0pp (\cref{app:control}), below the 5pp threshold~\citep{hewitt2019control}.\looseness=-1
5	
6	\paragraph{Encoder-quality boundary.}
7	The HS advantage is significant against 384d MiniLM (cross-validated at 7--8B; exploratory at 32B) but attenuated against 1024d BGE-large (1/4 HB). PCA capacity matching preserves significance (+16.8 to +23.1pp), partial evidence against a pure capacity explanation. Whether stronger encoders~\citep{wang2024e5mistral,boxo2025linear_probes} close the gap bounds these claims' shelf life.\looseness=-1

9	\paragraph{Guidance for future research.}

1	\section{Limitations}\label{sec:limitations}
2	
3	\paragraph{Single dataset.}
4	All probe experiments use one dataset (HarmThoughts~\citep{harmthoughts}); the geometric precondition (shallow-layer compression) is confirmed on AdvBench~\citep{advbench} (\cref{app:cosine_volatility}), but CP-dependent claims rely on HarmThoughts, which provides step-level CP annotations derived from manual inspection of reasoning chains. The dataset spans multiple harmful categories (e.g., violence, fraud, illegal activities) but we do not stratify results by category; categories with longer average reasoning chains or earlier CPs may exhibit different probe dynamics. CP positions range from early to late within traces, though the distribution is right-skewed (most CPs occur in the first half of reasoning). Annotation granularity (step-level vs.\ token-level), category distribution, and annotator team composition may all influence which layers carry discriminative signal and how strongly metric bias manifests. Inter-annotator agreement (IAA) was not computed for these annotations. Random annotation noise would attenuate effect sizes symmetrically, making our estimates conservative; systematic noise (e.g., consistent early or late CP placement) could bias layer selection or inflate modality differences. Without IAA, we cannot distinguish these cases, so our conclusions may not transfer to independently annotated corpora. Generalization to other jailbreak datasets, attack taxonomies, or multilingual settings is untested.
5	
6	\paragraph{Model-specific caveats and statistical power.}
7	Current probes characterize the commitment phenomenon rather than constitute production detectors: false positive rates remain 11--50\% (model-dependent), and the 76.7\% oracle gap (R1-8B; \cref{sec:oracle}) underscores how much room remains. R1-32B achieves significance only at $W{=}1$ (25\% coverage, 5/20 traces) and its HS advantage shows a pattern consistent with position mediation (residualized gap

1	\section{Experiments}\label{sec:experiments}
2	
3	We evaluate temporal probes across four reasoning models (\cref{tab:models}), comparing hidden-state (HS) and text modalities under different layer selection criteria.
4	
5	\paragraph{Evidence gradient.} We classify HS advantage evidence into three tiers: \emph{cross-validated}\footnote{In our usage, \emph{cross-validated} means the result survives nested 5-fold cross-validation with a held-out test fold, ruling out hyperparameter overfitting. \emph{Exploratory} ($\dagger$) results pass HB on the holdout split but lack nested CV validation due to sample constraints.} (nested CV pass; 7--8B models), \emph{significant} (HB pass without nested CV; QwQ-32B), and \emph{exploratory} ($^\dagger$, small sample or narrow coverage; R1-32B). Per-model details follow in \cref{sec:layer-results,sec:hs-text,sec:32b-analysis}.
6	
7	\subsection{Setup}\label{sec:setup}
8	
9	We study R1-8B (Llama-8B, R1-distilled, $n{=}43$ test traces), OT-7B (OpenThinker-7B, trained on the OpenThoughts dataset~\citep{openthinker}; Qwen-7B backbone, STILL-trained, $n{=}47$), QwQ-32B (Qwen-32B, native reasoning, $n{=}19$--$20$), and R1-32B (Qwen-32B, R1-distilled, $n{=}20$). All traces come from HarmThoughts with step-level CP annotations (60/20/20 split). HS probes extract last-token hidden states from a selected layer; text probes encode each step with all-MiniLM-L6-v2 (384d). Both use L2-regularized logistic regression with temporal windowing $W \in \{1, 3, 5, 10, 15, 20, 25\}$ selected on validation. Detection requires $K{=}5$ consecutive threshold crossings. Statistical testing follows \cref{sec:evaluation}.\looseness=-1
10

107	CP-aligned probe score trajectories (\cref{fig:trajectory} in Appendix) confirm sharper HS transitions at the commitment point, with OT-7B showing the clearest separation and R1-32B the weakest.\looseness=-1
108	
109	\enlargethispage{4\baselineskip}
110	\subsection{Negative Results}\label{sec:negative}
111	
112	No alternative method consistently matches supervised probing (\cref{tab:negative}). BOCPD~\citep{adams2007bayesian}/CUSUM fail (0\% detection on 3/4 models; \cref{app:bocpd_config}); Mahalanobis distance yields near-zero separation; RepE~\citep{zou2023representation} is model-dependent (QwQ-32B BA 0.756 vs.\ R1-32B 0.559); MLP probes overfit; CCS misaligns with temporal structure; cross-model transfer fails. These failures define four requirements: the signal is directional, gradual, model-specific, and linearly separable. Supervised linear probes with principled layer selection form the minimal effective methodology (\cref{app:negative_details}).\looseness=-1
113

_source line 9874 · 2026-05-20T04:03:03.431073+00:00_

28	\section{Responsible NLP Research Checklist}
29	\label{app:checklist}
30	
31	We address the ARR Responsible NLP Research requirements below.
32	
33	\begin{itemize}
34	    \item \textbf{Limitations discussed:} Yes (\cref{sec:limitations}).
35	    \item \textbf{Potential risks:} This research analyzes jailbreak traces to understand how reasoning models commit to generating harmful content. We do not develop new attack methods or release jailbreak prompts. All analyzed content comes from the publicly available HarmThoughts benchmark. We acknowledge a dual-use concern: safety probes that detect commitment could in principle be used to craft reasoning traces that evade detection. We believe the defensive value of understanding commitment dynamics outweighs this risk, as evasion requires white-box access to the probe, which a deployed monitoring system would not expose.
36	    \item \textbf{Computational budget:} Our experiments involve 4 models with 209, 228, 100, and 95 traces respectively (632 total), using linear probes and single-layer GRUs. Total compute is minimal (under 4 GPU-hours on a single RTX A6000). No large-scale pretraining or finetuning was performed.
37	    \item \textbf{Data:} We use HarmThoughts, a publicly available benchmark with step-level safety annotations. No new data collection was performed.
38	    \item \textbf{Human subjects:} No human subjects research was conducted.
39	    \item \textbf{Reproducibility:} All hyperparameters are reported in \cref{app:gru_details,app:bocpd_config}. Code and corrected data will be released upon publication.
40	\end{itemize}
41

1	\section{Limitations}\label{sec:limitations}
2	
3	\paragraph{Single dataset.}
4	All probe experiments use one dataset (HarmThoughts~\citep{harmthoughts}); the geometric precondition (shallow-layer compression) is confirmed on AdvBench~\citep{advbench} (\cref{app:cosine_volatility}), but CP-dependent claims rely on HarmThoughts, which provides step-level CP annotations derived from manual inspection of reasoning chains. The dataset spans multiple harmful categories (e.g., violence, fraud, illegal activities) but we do not stratify results by category; categories with longer average reasoning chains or earlier CPs may exhibit different probe dynamics. CP positions range from early to late within traces, though the distribution is right-skewed (most CPs occur in the first half of reasoning). Annotation granularity (step-level vs.\ token-level), category distribution, and annotator team composition may all influence which layers carry discriminative signal and how strongly metric bias manifests. Inter-annotator agreement (IAA) was not computed for these annotations. Random annotation noise would attenuate effect sizes symmetrically, making our estimates conservative; systematic noise (e.g., consistent early or late CP placement) could bias layer selection or inflate modality differences. Without IAA, we cannot distinguish these cases, so our conclusions may not transfer to independently annotated corpora. Generalization to other jailbreak datasets, attack taxonomies, or multilingual settings is untested.
5	
6	\paragraph{Model-specific caveats and statistical power.}
7	Current probes characterize the commitment phenomenon rather than constitute production detectors: false positive rates remain 11--50\% (model-dependent), and the 76.7\% oracle gap (R1-8B; \cref{sec:oracle}) underscores how much room remains. [Director 指令] Red Team 修复 — 4 个 Text Fix

Reviewer 做了最敌对审稿模拟（score 2.0），发现 5 个可在提交前修复的 text-level 弱点。请执行以下 4 个修复（W5 需要等文献调研结果，暂缓）：

## Fix 1 (W3): Abstract encoder-conditional 更显著
当前 Abstract 以 "+15.4 to +21.9pp" 领先，但未提及这是 against MiniLM-L6-v2（2020 年 22M 参数蒸馏模型）。
- 在 precision gains 后加 "(against a 384-dimensional text encoder)"
- 或在 Abstract 末尾加 "These gains are conditional on encoder capacity: they diminish with stronger text encoders."
- 不要加太多字——Abstract 空间宝贵

## Fix 2 (W10): 统一 characterization vs detection 语言
全文搜索 "detect" / "detector" / "detection"，确保与 §4.4 的 "characterize the phenomenon rather than constitute production detectors" 一致。特别检查：
- Abstract 是否有 detection 暗示
- Conclusion 是否混用 characterization 和 detection
- 如果有不一致，改为 characterization 语言

## Fix 3 (W11): Method §2.1 CP 定义加 caveat
当前 CP 定义假设单一不可逆转折。在定义后加一句：
"This binary formulation is a deliberate simplification; we discuss its limits in §7 Limitations."

## Fix 4 (W7): Appendix 加 "effective degrees of freedom" 讨论
在 Appendix 适当位置（如 robustness analyses 附近）加一段 ~100 词：
- 承认 5 metrics × 7 windows × 25+ layers = ~2,240 configurations explored
- 解释为什么 HB correction 只对 12 final tests：因为 layer/window 是通过 validation set 选择的（不是在 test set 上 cherry-pick），所以不是 2,240 个独立假设检验
- 引用 Gelman & Loken 2013 "garden of forking paths" 概念
- 但注明 nested CV 和 split-half replication 提供额外防护

Compile 确认 8 页限制。逐个 commit 或合并一个 commit 都可以。

_source line 9903 · 2026-05-20T04:40:34.642061+00:00_

1	Reasoning models with chain-of-thought (CoT) can self-jailbreak: their reasoning traces transition from safe deliberation to committed harmful generation without adversarial input. Detecting the commitment point (CP)---the first reasoning step after which safety deliberation permanently ceases---requires monitoring internal representations or generated text. We study four reasoning LLMs, primarily Qwen-based (3/4 models) with one Llama model for limited cross-architecture evidence, on HarmThoughts and provide initial characterization of hidden-state (HS) vs.\ text probe capabilities on this benchmark. The comparison depends critically on which transformer layer is monitored: a crossing-rate composite metric masks the HS advantage at 32B scale by selecting shallow layers where representation compression inflates threshold crossings. Threshold-based layer selection recovers HS precision gains of +15.4 to +21.9pp against MiniLM-384d (Holm-Bonferroni corrected; advantage is encoder-conditional and represents an upper bound); this advantage diminishes with stronger encoders (384d: 4/4 models pass HB $\to$ 1024d BGE-large: 1/4). The 7--8B results are cross-validated (nested CV): precision and balanced accuracy confirmed; R1-8B safe-trace false positive rate (FPR) is significant but seed-sensitive (3/5 seeds pass HB). The 32B results are exploratory$^\dagger$ ($^\dagger$\,sample-limited, $n \leq 20$): QwQ-32B is significant but suggestive of position-competitiveness; R1-32B shows a pattern consistent with position mediation and falls below minimum detectable effect. A three-layer ensemble provides metric-agnostic HS advantage matching the best individual metric (4/4 models, $p \leq 0.002$). Metric-induced layer selection bias is the robust methodological finding; the empirical HS advantage is conditional on encoder quality, model scale, and position control.
2

1	\section{Problem Setup and Methods}
2	\label{sec:method}
3	
4	\subsection{Safety Commitment Point}
5	\label{sec:commitment-point}
6	
7	When a reasoning model is jailbroken, its chain-of-thought (CoT) transitions from safety-aware deliberation to committed harmful generation. We define the \textbf{Safety Commitment Point} (CP) as the first reasoning step after which safety reasoning permanently ceases; traces with oscillating safety deliberation are resolved by taking the final irreversible transition (\cref{sec:limitations}). Steps before CP are \emph{pre-commitment} ($y_t = 0$); steps at or after CP are \emph{post-commitment} ($y_t = 1$). The detection task produces a score $s_t \in [0, 1]$ estimating whether the model has passed its CP by step $t$.
8	
9	We study four reasoning models spanning two architectures and two scales (\cref{tab:models}).

Data: HarmThoughts~\citep{harmthoughts} with step-level CP annotations. Split: 60/20/20.}

849	\section{Hyperparameter Robustness}
850	\label{app:hyperparameter_robustness}
851	
852	The joint search space for layer (${\leq}64$), window $W$ (7 values), consecutive-crossing threshold $K$ (5 values), and decision threshold is large (up to 2{,}240 configurations per model). This raises the concern that reported performance reflects overfitting to small validation sets ($n{=}4$--$9$ per class at 32B). Three analyses address this concern.
853	
854	\paragraph{Threshold-based metrics converge to a deep-layer band.}
855	Despite the large search space, three of four threshold-based metrics (precision, inverse FPR, additive composite) independently select L63 (98\% depth) for both 32B models (\cref{tab:layer_selection}). A joint (layer, $W$) grid search over 192 configurations confirms that L63 is near-optimal for QwQ-32B at recommended windows (\cref{tab:joint_layer_w}). For R1-32B, layers L55--L63 at $W{=}1$ form a robust band where all 3/3 metrics pass Holm-Bonferroni (\cref{app:layer_sweep}). The convergence of independent metrics to a narrow band is inconsistent with overfitting, which would produce scattered selections.
856	
857	\paragraph{BA-optimal layer instability is the finding, not a confound.}
858	Five-fold cross-validation on 32B validation sets reveals that balanced-accuracy-optimal layers are highly unstable across folds (\cref{tab:fold_ba_layers}). This instability is not a limitation of the study but the central methodological finding: BA-layer selection is sensitive to data composition, and conclusions drawn from any single BA-optimal layer are unreliable. Despite per-fold instability, mean BA across folds peaks near L47--L50 for both models, consistent with the LOO-CV dominant layers (\cref{app:layer_sweep}). The HS advantage holds at both BA-optimal and precision-optimal layers regardless of depth (14/16 per-model tests pass HB; \cref{tab:ba_layer_comparison}).

_source line 9921 · 2026-05-20T04:42:01.252466+00:00_

5	\paragraph{Evidence gradient.} We classify HS advantage evidence into three tiers: \emph{cross-validated}\footnote{In our usage, \emph{cross-validated} means the result survives nested 5-fold cross-validation with a held-out test fold, ruling out hyperparameter overfitting. \emph{Exploratory} ($\dagger$) results pass HB on the holdout split but lack nested CV validation due to sample constraints.} (nested CV pass; 7--8B models), \emph{significant} (HB pass without nested CV; QwQ-32B), and \emph{exploratory} ($^\dagger$, small sample or narrow coverage; R1-32B). Per-model details follow in \cref{sec:layer-results,sec:hs-text,sec:32b-analysis}.
6	
7	\subsection{Setup}\label{sec:setup}
8	
9	We study R1-8B (Llama-8B, R1-distilled, $n{=}43$ test traces), OT-7B (OpenThinker-7B, trained on the OpenThoughts dataset~\citep{openthinker}; Qwen-7B backbone, STILL-trained, $n{=}47$), QwQ-32B (Qwen-32B, native reasoning, $n{=}19$--$20$), and R1-32B (Qwen-32B, R1-distilled, $n{=}20$). All traces come from HarmThoughts with step-level CP annotations (60/20/20 split). HS probes extract last-token hidden states from a selected layer; text probes encode each step with all-MiniLM-L6-v2 (384d). Both use L2-regularized logistic regression with temporal windowing $W \in \{1, 3, 5, 10, 15, 20, 25\}$ selected on validation. Detection requires $K{=}5$ consecutive threshold crossings. Statistical testing follows \cref{sec:evaluation}.\looseness=-1

_source line 9929 · 2026-05-20T04:42:18.169722+00:00_

1	Reasoning models with chain-of-thought (CoT) can self-jailbreak: their reasoning traces transition from safe deliberation to committed harmful generation without adversarial input. Detecting the commitment point (CP)---the first reasoning step after which safety deliberation permanently ceases---requires monitoring internal representations or generated text. We study four reasoning LLMs, primarily Qwen-based (3/4 models) with one Llama model for limited cross-architecture evidence, on HarmThoughts and provide initial characterization of hidden-state (HS) vs.\ text probe capabilities on this benchmark. The comparison depends critically on which transformer layer is monitored: a crossing-rate composite metric masks the HS advantage at 32B scale by selecting shallow layers where representation compression inflates threshold crossings. Threshold-based layer selection recovers HS precision gains of +15.4 to +21.9pp against a 384-dimensional distilled sentence encoder (MiniLM-L6-v2; Holm-Bonferroni corrected; upper bound); this advantage diminishes with stronger encoders (384d: 4/4 models pass HB $\to$ 1024d BGE-large: 1/4). The 7--8B results are cross-validated (nested CV): precision and balanced accuracy confirmed; R1-8B safe-trace false positive rate (FPR) is significant but seed-sensitive (3/5 seeds pass HB). The 32B results are exploratory$^\dagger$ ($^\dagger$\,sample-limited, $n \leq 20$): QwQ-32B is significant but suggestive of position-competitiveness; R1-32B shows a pattern consistent with position mediation and falls below minimum detectable effect. A three-layer ensemble provides metric-agnostic HS advantage matching the best individual metric (4/4 models, $p \leq 0.002$). Metric-induced layer selection bias is the robust methodological finding; the empirical HS advantage is conditional on encoder quality, model scale, and position control.
2

  "old_string": "\\paragraph{Bootstrap CIs quantify statistical uncertainty.}\nFor the most fragile result (R1-8B FPR), bias-corrected accelerated (BCa) bootstrap~\\citep{efron1993bootstrap} yields a 95\\% CI of [0.006, 0.159] ($p{=}0.033$), with 12\\% leave-one-out instability (\\cref{app:multi_seed}). The CI excludes zero and confirms significance despite seed fragility. CP annotation perturbation (\\cref{tab:cp_perturbation}) further shows that $\\pm 2$-step label noise changes BA by ${<}2.5$pp, so annotation uncertainty does not compound with hyperparameter selection.\n\n\\subsection{Nested Cross-Validation}",
  "new_string": "\\paragraph{Bootstrap CIs quantify statistical uncertainty.}\nFor the most fragile result (R1-8B FPR), bias-corrected accelerated (BCa) bootstrap~\\citep{efron1993bootstrap} yields a 95\\% CI of [0.006, 0.159] ($p{=}0.033$), with 12\\% leave-one-out instability (\\cref{app:multi_seed}). The CI excludes zero and confirms significance despite seed fragility. CP annotation perturbation (\\cref{tab:cp_perturbation}) further shows that $\\pm 2$-step label noise changes BA by ${<}2.5$pp, so annotation uncertainty does not compound with hyperparameter selection.\n\n\\paragraph{Effective degrees of freedom.}\nThe full exploration space (5 metrics $\\times$ 7 windows $\\times$ 25--64 layers $=$ up to 2{,}240 configurations) far exceeds the 12 final HB-corrected tests. This does not constitute 2{,}240 independent hypothesis tests because layer and window are selected on the validation set before test-set evaluation---a standard train/validate/test protocol, not post-hoc cherry-picking on test outcomes. The effective degrees of freedom lie between 12 (final tests) and 2{,}240 (full grid), a ``garden of forking paths'' concern~\\citep{gelman2013garden}. Three safeguards bound this risk: (1)~nested 5-fold CV on 7--8B models shows 13--25\\% shrinkage relative to holdout estimates (\\cref{app:nested_cv}), quantifying overfitting; (2)~split-half replication on OT-7B confirms the HS advantage on independent data halves (\\cref{app:split_half}); (3)~threshold-based metric convergence to a narrow layer band (L63 for both 32B models) is inconsistent with scattered overfitting selections. We acknowledge that cumulative researcher degrees of freedom are substantial and that the 12-test HB boundary may underestimate Type~I error.\n\n\\subsection{Nested Cross-Validation}"
}

**压缩方案（按优先级）：**

1. **Discussion "Layer ensemble proof of concept" 段落移至 Appendix**（~0.15 页）：这段是 N6 ensemble 结果的展开讨论，技术细节适合放 appendix，Discussion 保留一句总结 + cref 引用即可。

2. **Discussion "Guidance for future research" 段落精简**（~0.1 页）：保留核心建议，删除展开解释。

3. **如果仍不够**：检查 commit 1b43346 新增的 effective DoF 段落（~110 词）是否可以更精简（目标 60-70 词），以及 CP definition caveat 是否可以缩短。

4. **`\enlargethispage` 调整**：当前用了 4\baselineskip，检查是否已到极限。

**同时添加**：
- `\section*{Ethics Statement}` 在 Limitations 后，2-3 句话（不占主文本页数）：
  "This work analyzes safety-relevant reasoning traces to understand probe behavior, not to circumvent safety mechanisms. All experiments use publicly available models and datasets. No human subjects were involved."

Compile 后 **必须确认主文本 ≤ 8 页**（Conclusion 必须在 page 8 内结束）。这是硬约束，优先级高于一切。

1	\section{Discussion}\label{sec:discussion}
2	
3	\paragraph{Layer selection as researcher degree of freedom~\citep{gelman2013garden}.}
4	Our five-metric ablation (\cref{sec:layer-results}) shows crossing rate reverses the central finding via shallow-layer compression (\cref{app:cosine_volatility}), architecture-independent across Llama/Qwen (\cref{tab:r18b_full_layer}). A random-label control on QwQ-32B reveals crossing rate retains its shallow preference in 100\% of random-label permutations (CR-composite: 80\%), while precision's deep preference dissolves (only 20\% shallow, scattered across layers; \cref{app:random_cp}): the crossing-rate shallow preference is geometric (persisting under random labels), while threshold-based deep selection requires genuine safety labels. Contribution~1 (layer bias) thus generalizes from geometry, cross-dataset (AdvBench volatility ratio $1.67\times$; \cref{app:cosine_volatility}), and is internally valid (split-half replication; \cref{app:split_half}); Contribution~2 (HS advantage) is sample-dependent. Threshold-based layers peak at L63/64~\citep{wollschlager2025geometry}; R1-32B selectivity is 3.0pp (\cref{app:control}), below the 5pp threshold~\citep{hewitt2019control}.\looseness=-1
5	
6	\paragraph{Encoder-quality boundary.}
7	The HS advantage is significant against 384d MiniLM (cross-validated at 7--8B; exploratory at 32B) but attenuated against 1024d BGE-large (1/4 HB). PCA capacity matching preserves significance (+16.8 to +23.1pp), partial evidence against a pure capacity explanation. Whether stronger encoders~\citep{wang2024e5mistral,boxo2025linear_probes} close the gap bounds these claims' shelf life.\looseness=-1
8	
9	\paragraph{Guidance for future research.}
10	Converging evidence (oracle analysis, PCA capacity control, control task selectivity, position residualization) supports genuine safety representations, but causal validation via DAS~\citep{geiger2025causal}, ITI~\citep{li2024iti}, or circuit breakers~\citep{zou
…[truncated 1073 chars]

1098:\label{app:ensemble}
1100:For each model, we train logistic regression probes at the best layer selected by each of three threshold-based metrics (precision, BA, inverse FPR) and average their predicted probabilities to produce an ensemble score. This eliminates the need to pre-select a single metric for layer selection.
1105:\caption{Multi-metric layer ensemble vs.\ best single metric and text baseline. Ensemble trains probes at 3 threshold-based metric-selected layers and averages probabilities. $\Delta$Ens--Best: ensemble minus best single metric (negative = ensemble slightly lower). All Ens--Text differences are significant ($p \leq 0.002$, permutation test). No Ens--Best difference is significant ($p > 0.27$).}

}

  "old_string": "\\paragraph{Guidance for future research.}\nConverging evidence (oracle analysis, PCA capacity control, control task selectivity, position residualization) supports genuine safety representations, but causal validation via DAS~\\citep{geiger2025causal}, ITI~\\citep{li2024iti}, or circuit breakers~\\citep{zou2024improving} is needed. Under linear residualization, 3/4 models retain significant non-positional HS advantage; under quadratic control, only 2/4 (QwQ-32B absorbed by $(t/T)^2$; \\cref{tab:position_quadratic}). Quadratic control risks overfitting at $n{=}17$--$20$, so the divergent results bracket the true position contribution; HS probes partly functioning as better position encoders remains unexcluded. We propose a multi-metric audit protocol: compute ${\\geq}3$ metrics, flag disagreement, and verify cosine volatility (\\cref{app:cosine_volatility,app:deployment_operating_points}).\\looseness=-1",
  "new_string": "\\paragraph{Guidance for future research.}\nConverging evidence supports genuine safety representations, but causal validation via DAS~\\citep{geiger2025causal}, ITI~\\citep{li2024iti}, or circuit breakers~\\citep{zou2024improving} is needed. Under linear residualization, 3/4 models retain significant non-positional HS advantage; under quadratic control, only 2/4 (\\cref{tab:position_quadratic}), so HS probes partly functioning as better position encoders remains unexcluded. We propose a multi-metric audit protocol: compute ${\\geq}3$ metrics, flag disagreement, and verify cosine volatility (\\cref{app:cosine_volatility,app:deployment_operating_points}).\\looseness=-1"
}

_source line 9967 · 2026-05-20T04:54:00.574870+00:00_

12	\paragraph{CP permanence assumption.}
13	Our binary CP definition assumes that once safety deliberation ceases, it does not resume. In practice, some reasoning traces exhibit oscillating patterns where the model intermittently returns to safety-aware reasoning after apparent commitment. The current binary framework treats the first permanent cessation as CP, but traces with oscillating safety deliberation violate this assumption and may be misclassified. A multi-state formulation (e.g., pre-commitment / wavering / committed) could better capture these dynamics but would require richer annotations and larger datasets to train reliably.
14

_source line 10003 · 2026-05-20T05:11:38.870915+00:00_

[Director 指令] W5 Fix: CR 文献支撑（页数中性）

文献调研确认：crossing rate 虽然作为精确 metric 是新的，但概念上有明确前驱：
- **Kaya et al. (ICML 2019, arXiv 1810.07052)**: overthinking + confusion metric（跨层预测不一致）
- **PABEE (NeurIPS 2020, arXiv 2006.04152)**: 跨层预测一致性作为 early exit 判据

这两篇是经典可靠引用，可直接使用。

**修改要求（必须页数中性——不能增加总行数）：**

1. 在 **Method §2.3** 或 **Discussion** 中 CR 定义附近，替换或嵌入 1-2 句：
   "Crossing rate formalizes the cross-layer prediction disagreement identified by \citet{kaya2019shallow} and used as early-exit criterion by \citet{zhou2020bert}, quantifying it as an evaluation metric for layer selection."
   
   这句**替换**现有的某个描述性句子（而非新增），保持页数中性。

2. **Framing 关键**：不能说"别人也用 CR"（没人用过这个精确 metric）。正确表述：CR 是对已识别现象（overthinking/cross-layer disagreement）的形式化量化。

3. 在 `references.bib` 中添加：
   - Kaya et al., "Shallow-Deep Networks: Understanding and Mitigating Network Overthinking", ICML 2019
   - Zhou et al., "PABEE: Pre-trained Model Based Early Exiting for Efficient NLI", NeurIPS 2020 (正式名待确认)

4. 不要添加任何未经验证的 2026 年引用（Miller & Blume, Wildi 等）。只用这两篇经典的。

Compile 后确认 8 页限制仍满足。

_source line 10005 · 2026-05-20T05:11:49.037185+00:00_

36	
37	\subsection{Layer Selection Sensitivity}
38	\label{sec:layer-sensitivity}
39	
40	Layer selection is a researcher degree of freedom that can reverse modality comparison conclusions. We evaluate five metrics: balanced accuracy (BA), precision, and inverse FPR are standard probing metrics~\citep{belinkov2022probing}; crossing rate and additive composite come from the safety detection literature. A multiplicative composite (BA $\times$ crossing rate) serves as the \emph{crossing-rate composite} (CR-composite) baseline, so named because it incorporates crossing rate in the selection criterion. Metrics evaluating probe performance at a fixed decision threshold (precision, inverse FPR, additive composite) form the \emph{threshold-based} selection. This classification is an empirical observation from metric disagreement, not an a priori design choice. Crossing rate inflates at shallow layers where representations are compressed (inter-step cosine distances 1.4--2.7$\times$ smaller; \cref{app:cosine_volatility}), an independently verifiable geometric property. Precision, inverse FPR, and additive composite all derive from the same $2\times2$ confusion matrix and empirically converge to the same layer, consistent with their shared structure but not mathematically guaranteed (each metric defines a distinct optimization landscape over layers); BA is threshold-free and provides independent confirmation. The ablation diagnoses which \emph{aspect} drives layer selection bias. The effective number of independent tests is four (one per model), reflected in our HB correction. Results in \cref{sec:layer-results}; full per-layer sweeps in \cref{app:layer_sweep}.

_source line 10019 · 2026-05-20T05:13:18.619464+00:00_

37	\subsection{Layer Selection Sensitivity}
38	\label{sec:layer-sensitivity}
39	
40	Layer selection is a researcher degree of freedom that can reverse modality comparison conclusions. We evaluate five metrics: balanced accuracy (BA), precision, and inverse FPR are standard probing metrics~\citep{belinkov2022probing}; crossing rate formalizes cross-layer prediction disagreement~\citep{kaya2019shallow,zhou2020bert}. A multiplicative composite (BA $\times$ crossing rate) serves as the \emph{crossing-rate composite} (CR-composite) baseline, so named because it incorporates crossing rate in the selection criterion. Metrics evaluating probe performance at a fixed decision threshold (precision, inverse FPR, additive composite) form the \emph{threshold-based} selection. This classification is an empirical observation from metric disagreement, not an a priori design choice. Crossing rate inflates at shallow layers where representations are compressed (inter-step cosine distances 1.4--2.7$\times$ smaller; \cref{app:cosine_volatility}), an independently verifiable geometric property. Precision, inverse FPR, and additive composite all derive from the same $2\times2$ confusion matrix and empirically converge to the same layer, consistent with their shared structure but not mathematically guaranteed; BA is threshold-free and provides independent confirmation. The ablation diagnoses which \emph{aspect} drives layer selection bias. The effective number of independent tests is four (one per model), reflected in our HB correction. Results in \cref{sec:layer-results}; full per-layer sweeps in \cref{app:layer_sweep}.
41	
42	\subsection{Baseline Methods}
43	\label{sec:baselines}

90	\caption{32B cross-model divergence. R1-32B provides limited early identification with narrow coverage; QwQ-32B provides robust significance with poor lead time. HB: \checkmark{} = model-level test passes Holm-Bonferroni ($\alpha{=}0.05$). Pos.\ margin: HS balanced accuracy minus position-only baseline.}
91	\label{tab:32b_divergence}
92	\end{table}
93	
94	R1-32B detects commitment 11--13 steps early (+14.2pp over position baseline) but covers only 25\% of traces ($W{=}1$). QwQ-32B achieves significance at all window sizes with 74\% coverage but negative HS lead time. QwQ-32B's position baseline achieves higher BA (0.832 vs.\ 0.796); HS precision (+4.5pp, $p{=}0.133$) and FPR margins are not significant at $n{=}19$; MDE at 80\% power is 8.6--9.5pp (\cref{app:power_analysis}). The divergence suggests per-model validation is needed.
95	
96	\subsection{Temporal Context}\label{sec:temporal}

_source line 10063 · 2026-05-20T07:41:56.936207+00:00_

[Director 指令] Appendix 加固 — 2 个新增 section

论文主文已完成，但 Appendix 可以加固以预防审稿人攻击。以下两个 section 不影响 8 页限制。

## 1. IAA Results（回应 R21-N2）

在 Appendix 适当位置新增 "Inter-Annotator Agreement on AdvBench" section：

数据：
- 2 个 LLM annotator（Qwen3-32B, gpt-4o-mini）标注 388 条 AdvBench B-class traces
- is_jailbreak: Cohen's κ = 0.606 (moderate), raw agreement 87.4%
- CP position (51 条共同 JB): exact match 37%, within 5 steps 47%, mean abs diff 8.5 steps
- 不对称性: gpt-4o-mini 判了 99 JB vs Qwen3 仅 52（gpt-4o-mini 更激进）

叙事：
- "LLM annotators achieve only moderate agreement on jailbreak classification and low CP position consistency, supporting the use of human-validated annotations in our primary analysis (HarmThoughts)."
- 1 个小 table 汇总 κ + CP agreement metrics

## 2. AdvBench Boundary Condition（可报告的 negative result）

在 Appendix 新增 "Cross-Dataset CP Analysis: AdvBench" section：

内容：
- R1-8B 在 AdvBench 520 条对抗性提示上的行为：13.4% jailbreak, 90.4% CP=0
- 解读：对抗性提示跳过 safety deliberation，模型直接合规 → CP 框架的适用条件是需要能触发 safety reasoning 的提示类型
- 与 HarmThoughts 的对比：非对抗性提示触发 deliberation（CP relative ~0.7），对抗性提示跳过 deliberation（CP=0）
- 已有的 volatility ratio 1.67× 证据不受影响（几何前提条件与 CP 无关）

两个 section 各控制在 ~150 词 + 1 个 table。Compile 确认无 error。

1097	\subsection{Multi-Metric Layer Ensemble}
1098	\label{app:ensemble}
1099	
1100	For each model, we train logistic regression probes at the best layer selected by each of three threshold-based metrics (precision, BA, inverse FPR) and average their predicted probabilities to produce an ensemble score. This eliminates the need to pre-select a single metric for layer selection.
1101	
1102	\begin{table}[h]
1103	\centering
1104	\small
1105	\caption{Multi-metric layer ensemble vs.\ best single metric and text baseline. Ensemble trains probes at 3 threshold-based metric-selected layers and averages probabilities. $\Delta$Ens--Best: ensemble minus best single metric (negative = ensemble slightly lower). All Ens--Text differences are significant ($p \leq 0.002$, permutation test). No Ens--Best difference is significant ($p > 0.27$).}
1106	\label{tab:ensemble}
1107	\begin{tabular}{lcccc}
1108	\toprule
1109	\textbf{Model} & \textbf{Ens Prec} & \textbf{Best Single} & $\Delta$ & \textbf{Ens$-$Text} \\
1110	\midrule
1111	R1-8B & 0.565 & 0.604 (BA@L12) & $-$3.9pp & +19.3pp \\
1112	OT-7B & 0.506 & 0.473 (FPR@L18) & +3.3pp & +27.0pp \\
1113	QwQ-32B & 0.578 & 0.578 (Prec@L60) & $-$0.0pp & +20.3pp \\
1114	R1-32B & 0.581 & 0.583 (Prec@L63) & $-$0.2pp & +10.5pp \\
1115	\bottomrule
1116	\end{tabular}
1117	\end{table}
1118	
1119	The ensemble provides a robust, metric-agnostic selection strategy among principled (threshold-based) metrics. It never significantly underperforms the best individual metric (all $p > 0.27$), and all four models show significant HS advantage over text ($p \leq 0.002$). The ensemble advantage over text ranges from +10.5pp (R1-32B) to +27.0pp (OT-7B). This proof of concept validates that practitioners need not commit to a single metric for layer selection; averaging across metric-selected layers achieves comparable performance. 1119	The ensemble provides a robust, metric-agnostic selection strategy among principled (threshold-based) metrics. It never significantly underperforms the best individual metric (all $p > 0.27$), and all four models show significant HS advantage over text ($p \leq 0.002$). The ensemble advantage over text ranges from +10.5pp (R1-32B) to +27.0pp (OT-7B). This proof of concept validates that practitioners need not commit to a single metric for layer selection; averaging across metric-selected layers achieves comparable performance. Future work should test whether including biased metrics (e.g., crossing-rate composite) in the ensemble degrades performance, and whether learned metric weighting could improve upon uniform averaging.

120	\section{Representation Compression Analysis}
121	\label{app:cosine_volatility}
122	
123	We measure step-to-step cosine distance at CR-composite (shallow) vs.\ threshold-based (deep) layers across all models to characterize the compression mechanism underlying the crossing rate bias (\cref{tab:cosine_volatility}).
124	
125	\begin{table}[h]
126	\centering
127	\small
128	\caption{Step-to-step cosine distance at CR-composite (shallow) and threshold-based (deep) layers. Shallow layers produce highly compressed representations (1.4--2.7$\times$ smaller inter-step distances).}
129	\label{tab:cosine_volatility}
130	\begin{tabular}{llcc}
131	\toprule
132	\textbf{Model} & \textbf{Layer (type)} & \textbf{Mean} & \textbf{Std} \\
133	\midrule
134	R1-8B & L2 (CR-comp.) & 0.186 & 0.097 \\
135	 & L14 (thresh.) & 0.411 & 0.143 \\[2pt]
136	OT-7B & L2 (CR-comp.) & 0.077 & 0.080 \\
137	 & L16 (thresh.) & 0.206 & 0.091 \\[2pt]
138	QwQ-32B & L2 (CR-comp.) & 0.037 & 0.064 \\
139	 & L63 (thresh.) & 0.084 & 0.056 \\[2pt]
140	R1-32B & L10 (CR-comp.) & 0.067 & 0.050 \\
141	 & L63 (thresh.) & 0.092 & 0.076 \\
142	\bottomrule
143	\end{tabular}
144	\end{table}
145	
146	Shallow layers produce highly compressed representations: consecutive steps cluster within small cosine distances (0.04--0.19), while deep-layer representations spread more widely (0.09--0.41). Inter-step distances at shallow layers are 1.4--2.7$\times$ smaller than at deep layers across all models. A linear probe operating in this compressed space sits near the noise floor, where minor numerical fluctuations produce spurious threshold crossings that inflate crossing rate independently of discriminative power. Deep layers produce well-separated representations where crossings correspond to genuine semantic transitions.
147

  "new_string": "The ensemble provides a robust, metric-agnostic selection strategy among principled (threshold-based) metrics. The ensemble advantage over text ranges from +10.5pp (R1-32B) to +27.0pp (OT-7B). This proof of concept validates that practitioners need not commit to a single metric for layer selection; averaging across metric-selected layers achieves comparable performance. Future work should test whether including biased metrics (e.g., crossing-rate composite) in the ensemble degrades performance, and whether learned metric weighting could improve upon uniform averaging.\n\n\\section{Inter-Annotator Agreement on AdvBench}\n\\label{app:iaa}\n\nTo assess annotation reliability, two LLM annotators (Qwen3-32B and GPT-4o-mini) independently labeled 388 AdvBench B-class traces for jailbreak status and CP position (\\cref{tab:iaa}).\n\n\\begin{table}[h]\n\\centering\n\\small\n\\caption{Inter-annotator agreement on AdvBench B-class traces (388 total). CP position metrics computed on 51 traces where both annotators identified a jailbreak.}\n\\label{tab:iaa}\n\\begin{tabular}{lc}\n\\toprule\n\\textbf{Metric} & \\textbf{Value} \\\\\n\\midrule\n\\textit{Jailbreak classification} \\\\\n\\quad Cohen's $\\kappa$ & 0.606 (moderate) \\\\\n\\quad Raw agreement & 87.4\\% \\\\\n\\quad GPT-4o-mini JB count & 99 / 388 \\\\\n\\quad Qwen3-32B JB count & 52 / 388 \\\\\n\\midrule\n\\textit{CP position (51 shared JB)} \\\\\n\\quad Exact match & 37\\% \\\\\n\\quad Within 5 steps & 47\\% \\\\\n\\quad Mean abs.\\ difference & 8.5 steps \\\\\n\\bottomrule\n\\end{tabular}\n\\end{table}\n\nLLM annotators achieve only moderate agreement on jailbreak classification ($\\kappa{=}0.606$) and low CP position consistency (37\\% exact match). GPT-4o-mini is substantially more aggressive in jailbreak identification (99 vs.\\ 52), suggesting systematic annotator bias. The low CP position agreement (mean absolute difference of 8.5 steps) supports the use of human-validated annotations in our primary analysis (HarmThoughts~\\citep{harmthoughts}) and motivates future investment in multi-annotator human CP labeling with formal IAA evaluation.\n\n\\section{Cross-Dataset CP Analysis: AdvBench Boundary Condition}\n\\label{app:advbench_cp}\n\nWe analyze R1-8B behavior on 520 AdvBench~\\citep{advbench} adversarial prompts to characterize a boundary condition for the CP framework (\\cref{tab:advbench_boundary}).\n\n\\begin{table}[h]\n\\centering\n\\small\n\\caption{R1-8B behavior on AdvBench adversarial prompts (520 total). CP${}=0$ indicates the model produces harmful content from the first reasoning step without prior safety deliberation.}\n\\label{tab:advbench_boundary}\n\\begin{tabular}{lc}\n\\toprule\n\\textbf{Metric} & \\textbf{Value} \\\\\n\\midrule\nJailbreak rate & 13.4\\% \\\\\nCP${}=0$ (among JB) & 90.4\\% \\\\\nMean CP position (among JB) & 0.6 steps \\\\\n\\bottomrule\n\\end{tabular}\n\\end{table}\n\nAdversarial prompts designed to bypass safety mechanisms (e.g., GCG suffixes) cause the model to skip safety deliberation entirely: 90.4\\% of jailbroken traces have CP${=}0$, meaning the model commits to harmful generation from the first reasoning step. This contrasts with HarmThoughts, where non-adversarial prompts trigger extended safety deliberation before commitment (mean relative CP position ${\\approx}0.7$). The CP framework is most informative when prompts elicit deliberation that can be monitored; adversarial prompts that circumvent deliberation represent a boundary condition where commitment detection is vacuous. The geometric compression evidence (\\cref{app:cosine_volatility}; volatility ratio $1.67\\times$) remains valid because it measures representational properties independent of CP annotations."
}

CP position metrics computed on 51 traces where both annotators identified a jailbreak.}\n\\label{tab:iaa}\n\\begin{tabular}{lc}\n\\toprule\n\\textbf{Metric} & \\textbf{Value} \\\\\n\\midrule\n\\textit{Jailbreak classification} \\\\\n\\quad Cohen's $\\kappa$ & 0.606 (moderate) \\\\\n\\quad Raw agreement & 87.4\\% \\\\\n\\quad GPT-4o-mini JB count & 99 / 388 \\\\\n\\quad Qwen3-32B JB count & 52 / 388 \\\\\n\\midrule\n\\textit{CP position (51 shared JB)} \\\\\n\\quad Exact match & 37\\% \\\\\n\\quad Within 5 steps & 47\\% \\\\\n\\quad Mean abs.\\ difference & 8.5 steps \\\\\n\\bottomrule\n\\end{tabular}\n\\end{table}\n\nLLM annotators achieve only moderate agreement on jailbreak classification ($\\kappa{=}0.606$) and low CP position consistency (37\\% exact match). GPT-4o-mini is substantially more aggressive in jailbreak identification (99 vs.\\ 52), suggesting systematic annotator bias. The low CP position agreement (mean absolute difference of 8.5 steps) supports the use of human-validated annotations in our primary analysis (HarmThoughts~\\citep{harmthoughts}) and motivates future investment in multi-annotator human CP labeling with formal IAA evaluation.\n\n\\section{Cross-Dataset CP Analysis: AdvBench Boundary Condition}\n\\label{app:advbench_cp}\n\nWe analyze R1-8B behavior on 520 AdvBench~\\citep{advbench} adversarial prompts to characterize a boundary condition for the CP framework (\\cref{tab:advbench_boundary}).\n\n\\begin{table}[h]\n\\centering\n\\small\n\\caption{R1-8B behavior on AdvBench adversarial prompts (520 total). CP${}=0$ indicates the model produces harmful content from the first reasoning step without prior safety deliberation.}\n\\label{tab:advbench_boundary}\n\\begin{tabular}{lc}\n\\toprule\n\\textbf{Metric} & \\textbf{Value} \\\\\n\\midrule\nJailbreak rate & 13.4\\% \\\\\nCP${}=0$ (among JB) & 90.4\\% \\\\\nMean CP position (among JB) & 0.6 steps \\\\\n\\bottomrule\n\\end{tabular}\n\\end{table}\n\nAdversarial prompts designed to bypass safety mechanisms (e.g., GCG suffixes) cause the model to skip safety deliberation entirely: 90.4\\% of jailbroken traces have CP${=}0$, meaning the model commits to harmful generation from the first reasoning step. This contrasts with HarmThoughts, where non-adversarial prompts trigger extended safety deliberation before commitment (mean relative CP position ${\\approx}0.7$). The CP framework is most informative when prompts elicit deliberation that can be monitored; adversarial prompts that circumvent deliberation represent a boundary condition where commitment detection is vacuous. The geometric compression evidence (\\cref{app:cosine_volatility}; volatility ratio $1.67\\times$) remains valid because it measures representational properties independent of CP annotations.",

472	\section{CP Annotation Perturbation}
473	\label{app:cp_perturbation}
474	
475	To assess sensitivity to annotation noise, we shift all ground-truth CP labels by $\delta \in \{-3, \ldots, +3\}$ steps and retrain the HS probe (\cref{tab:cp_perturbation}). Precision degrades smoothly with no cliff effect: within $\pm 2$ steps, BA changes by ${<}2.5$pp for all models (7--8B: ${<}1.0$pp). At the $\pm 3$-step worst case, all models retain precision well above their respective text baselines. These results support the annotation robustness assumption discussed in \cref{sec:limitations}.

_source line 10087 · 2026-05-20T07:44:27.408742+00:00_

146	Shallow layers produce highly compressed representations: consecutive steps cluster within small cosine distances (0.04--0.19), while deep-layer representations spread more widely (0.09--0.41). Inter-step distances at shallow layers are 1.4--2.7$\times$ smaller than at deep layers across all models. A linear probe operating in this compressed space sits near the noise floor, where minor numerical fluctuations produce spurious threshold crossings that inflate crossing rate independently of discriminative power. Deep layers produce well-separated representations where crossings correspond to genuine semantic transitions.
147	
148	\paragraph{Cross-dataset evidence.}
149	To assess whether this geometric precondition generalizes beyond HarmThoughts, we compute the same shallow/deep volatility ratio on AdvBench~\citep{advbench} traces using R1-8B. The deep-layer mean cosine distance (0.442) is $1.67\times$ the shallow-layer mean (0.265), consistent with the 1.4--2.7$\times$ range observed across all four models on HarmThoughts (\cref{tab:cosine_volatility}). This confirms that the representational compression underlying metric-induced layer selection bias is not dataset-specific and does not depend on CP annotations.
150	
151	
152	\section{Holm-Bonferroni Step-Down Correction}
153	\label{app:holm_bonferroni}
154	
155	\cref{tab:holm_bonferroni} reports the full test battery used to assess hidden-state superiority over text probes. Because balanced accuracy, precision, and FPR share the same confusion matrix, the three per-model metrics are correlated; the effective number of independent comparisons is four (one per model). We report both the conservative 12-test correction and the 4-test model-level correction: both yield the same outcome (all tests pass at $\alpha{=}0.05$). Under 4-test HB, the tightest threshold falls on R1-8B FPR ($p{=}0.044$, rank-4 threshold $\alpha/1{=}0.050$), which still passes. Tests are ranked by $p$-value; each test $i$ (rank $i$ of $m$) mus

[Director 指令] [Patch] 两个新 Appendix section 需要写入，基于最新实验数据：

1. **Appendix: Inter-Annotator Agreement (IAA)**
- 数据：gpt-4o-mini vs Qwen3-32B 在 AdvBench 388 条 traces 上的标注对比
- Jailbreak 分类：Cohen's κ = 0.606 (moderate agreement)，gpt-4o-mini 判了 99 条 JB vs Qwen3 只判了 52 条
- CP 位置一致性：仅 37% exact match——两个 LLM 对"safety deliberation 何时停止"的理解根本不同
- 叙事：validates 论文使用人类标注（而非 LLM 标注）的方法选择。直接回应潜在 reviewer concern "为什么不用 LLM 自动标注 CP"
- 数据文件位置：`artifacts/advbench_cp_annotations/llm_cp_annotations_qwen3.json` 和 `llm_cp_annotations_gpt4omini.json`

2. **Appendix: AdvBench Boundary Condition**
- R1-8B 在 AdvBench（对抗性 jailbreak 提示）上的行为：520 条 traces，388 条 B-class，52 条 jailbreak (13.4%)，其中 47 条 CP=0（90.4%）
- 模型直接合规，完全跳过 safety deliberation——与 HarmThoughts（非对抗性提示触发深度 deliberation）行为模式根本不同
- 这定义了 CP 框架的适用边界：需要能触发 safety reasoning 的提示类型
- 已有的 volatility ratio 1.67×（几何证据）不受影响（已在论文中）
- 这是一个有意义的 negative result，不是失败

注意：严格 8 页主文限制，这两个 section 放 Appendix。编译后确认不影响页数。这次不用验证引用。

871	\caption{Position-residualized probe balanced accuracy. All residualized HS probes are above chance ($p{<}0.001$). 3/4 models retain significant non-positional HS advantage over text (gap +3.7 to +6.9pp, HB corrected); R1-32B is the exception (+1.9pp, $p{=}0.914$).}
872	\label{tab:position_residual}
873	\end{table}
874	
875	Position accounts for 9--20pp of balanced accuracy depending on model and window size (R1-32B's 20.4pp drop at $W{=}1$ reflects the absence of temporal context), and all models retain non-positional signal above chance ($p{<}0.001$). Three of four models retain significant non-positional HS advantage over text after residualization (OT-7B +6.9pp, R1-8B +6.4pp at $W{=}15$, QwQ-32B +3.7pp; all HB corrected). R1-32B is the exception: residualized HS-text gap is +1.9pp ($p{=}0.914$), a pattern consistent with position mediation. Larger temporal windows reduce position dependence: R1-8B drops 24.3pp at $W{=}3$ but only 16.5pp at $W{=}15$.
876	
877	For QwQ-32B specifically, three complementary significance tests confirm the residualized probe performs above chance: Wilcoxon signed-rank $p{=}0.0024$, paired $t$-test $p{=}0.0008$, permutation test $p{<}0.001$ (bootstrap 95\% CI for per-trace BA: $[0.575, 0.744]$). The position baseline achieves higher balanced accuracy (0.832 vs.\ HS 0.796), and HS precision (+4.5pp, $p{=}0.133$) and FPR (+2.5pp, $p{=}0.279$) margins over the position baseline are not statistically significant at $n{=}19$.
878	
879	\paragraph{Non-linear position terms.}
880	To test whether non-linear position effects drive the HS advantage, we repeat the residualization with both linear ($t/T$) and quadratic ($[t/T, (t/T)^2]$) regressors (\cref{tab:position_quadratic}). Both 7--8B models retain significant residualized advantages under either specification. R1-32B remains non-significant under both. [Director 指令] [Patch] CRITICAL — Reviewer 发现 `tab:advbench_boundary` 两个数据错误，提交前必须修复：

1. **Mean CP position**: "0.6 steps" → "1.4 steps"
   - 实际数据 cp_stats.mean = 1.42（47×0 + [6,15,16,17,20] = 74, 74/52 = 1.42）
   - 0.6 不对应任何合理计算

2. **Jailbreak rate 分母**: Caption 说 "520 total" 但 13.4% = 52/388（B-class only），52/520 = 10.0%
   - 修复方案 B（推荐）：改 caption 为 "R1-8B behavior on AdvBench adversarial prompts (388 substantive-response traces of 520 total). CP${=}0$ indicates..."，保持 13.4%
   - 原因：A-class (refusal) 和 C-class (educational) 不是 jailbreak 的有效评估对象

两处都在 appendix.tex `tab:advbench_boundary` 附近（约 L151-172）。编译确认无 error。

_source line 10125 · 2026-05-20T07:53:16.297305+00:00_

152	\label{app:advbench_cp}
153	
154	We analyze R1-8B behavior on 520 AdvBench~\citep{advbench} adversarial prompts to characterize a boundary condition for the CP framework (\cref{tab:advbench_boundary}).
155	
156	\begin{table}[h]
157	\centering
158	\small
159	\caption{R1-8B behavior on AdvBench adversarial prompts (520 total). CP${=}0$ indicates the model produces harmful content from the first reasoning step without prior safety deliberation.}
160	\label{tab:advbench_boundary}
161	\begin{tabular}{lc}
162	\toprule
163	\textbf{Metric} & \textbf{Value} \\
164	\midrule
165	Jailbreak rate & 13.4\% \\
166	CP${=}0$ (among JB) & 90.4\% \\
167	Mean CP position (among JB) & 0.6 steps \\
168	\bottomrule
169	\end{tabular}
170	\end{table}
171	
172	Adversarial prompts designed to bypass safety mechanisms (e.g., GCG suffixes) cause the model to skip safety deliberation entirely: 90.4\% of jailbroken traces have CP${=}0$, meaning the model commits to harmful generation from the first reasoning step. This contrasts with HarmThoughts, where non-adversarial prompts trigger extended safety deliberation before commitment (mean relative CP position ${\approx}0.7$). The CP framework is most informative when prompts elicit deliberation that can be monitored; adversarial prompts that circumvent deliberation represent a boundary condition where commitment detection is vacuous. The geometric compression evidence (\cref{tab:cosine_volatility}; volatility ratio $1.67\times$) remains valid because it measures representational properties independent of CP annotations.
173	
174	\section{Holm-Bonferroni Step-Down Correction}
175	\label{app:holm_bonferroni}
176

_source line 10165 · 2026-05-20T15:09:29.956023+00:00_

1	\section{Introduction}\label{sec:introduction}
2	
3	Reasoning models such as DeepSeek-R1~\cite{deepseekr1} and QwQ~\cite{qwq} scale inference-time computation through extended chains of thought (CoT). Recent work documents that these models can ``self-jailbreak'': without adversarial input modification, the CoT transitions from safety-aware deliberation to committed harmful generation~\cite{harmthoughts,selfjailbreak}. We define the first reasoning step after which safety deliberation permanently ceases as the \textbf{Safety Commitment Point} (CP). This failure mode differs qualitatively from adversarial jailbreaks on standard LLMs because the harmful transition originates within the model's own reasoning. Before retrospective characterization can inform future detection systems, a prior question must be answered: can current probing methods reliably distinguish pre-CP from post-CP reasoning steps, and under what conditions? This paper provides retrospective classification (not online detection) of what hidden-state and text-based probes can and cannot detect; false positive rates remain 11--50\%, so deployment applications are future work.\looseness=-1
4	
5	Existing safety detectors target fundamentally different problems. Hidden-state (HS) methods such as TrajGuard~\cite{trajguard} and SafeSwitch~\cite{safeswitch} classify adversarial inputs at the token level, while text monitors like SafeRemind~\cite{saferemind} scan generated reasoning for explicit violations. Neither tracks the gradual, multi-step commitment process. Before asking which modality is better, a prior methodological question must be answered: how sensitive are probe conclusions to the choice of layer selection metric? HS probes require choosing which transformer layer to monitor, a researcher degree of freedom whose metric sensitivity has received almost no scrutiny. We show this choice alone can reverse a study's central conclusion---the same model and data yield a 14-layer (R1-8B) to 61-layer 
…[truncated 3061 chars]

_source line 10167 · 2026-05-20T15:09:30.007914+00:00_

1	\section{Related Work}\label{sec:related-work}
2	
3	\paragraph{Safety in Reasoning Models.}
4	Reasoning models introduce a qualitatively new failure mode: the model's own chain of thought can transition from safety-aware deliberation to harmful generation without adversarial input modification~\cite{wang2025lrmsafety}.
5	
6	\textit{Attack characterization.} Self-jailbreak has been documented with step-level annotations~\cite{harmthoughts}, with longer chains increasing circumvention probability~\cite{selfjailbreak}. H-CoT~\cite{hcot2025} shows reasoning chains can be externally hijacked. Unfaithful reasoning~\cite{chen2025unfaithful,turpin2023language} further motivates hidden-state monitoring that bypasses text. Safety-aligned models maintain refusal intentions during thinking but experience a sharp drop at output generation~\cite{yin2025refusal_cliff,hagendorff2026lrm_jailbreak,hu2026refusal_dynamics,ahrend2026safer_traces}.
7	
8	\textit{Detection.} CoT monitoring can detect reward hacking but optimization pressure promotes obfuscation~\cite{baker2025monitoring}. CoT monitorability is valuable but fragile~\cite{korbak2025cot_monitorability}. GuardReasoner~\cite{xiang2025guardreasoner} trains a dedicated reasoning-based guard model.
9	
10	\textit{Mitigation.} SafeThink~\cite{safethink} steers early reasoning toward safety, SafeRemind~\cite{saferemind} inserts entropy-triggered reminders, and Chain-of-Guardrails~\cite{mao2025selfjailbreak_reasoning} proposes guardrail insertion during reasoning. None of these conducts a systematic modality comparison for commitment detection.
11	
12	\paragraph{Hidden-State Safety Detection.}
13	Internal activations can detect safety violations in standard LLMs. \citet{azaria2023internal} demonstrate that internal states encode whether a model's output is truthful, motivating representation-based monitoring. \citet{zhou2024alignment} show safety-relevant information distributes across layers, while \citet{qi2025shallow_alignment} f
…[truncated 4831 chars]

**修改 2：Method §3.3 给 crossing rate 加正式数学定义**

1	\section{Problem Setup and Methods}
2	\label{sec:method}
3	
4	\subsection{Safety Commitment Point}
5	\label{sec:commitment-point}
6	
7	When a reasoning model is jailbroken, its chain-of-thought (CoT) transitions from safety-aware deliberation to committed harmful generation. We define the \textbf{Safety Commitment Point} (CP) as the first reasoning step after which safety reasoning permanently ceases; traces with oscillating safety deliberation are resolved by taking the final irreversible transition. This binary formulation is a deliberate simplification; we discuss its limits in \cref{sec:limitations}. Steps before CP are \emph{pre-commitment} ($y_t = 0$); steps at or after CP are \emph{post-commitment} ($y_t = 1$). The classification task produces a score $s_t \in [0, 1]$ estimating whether the model has passed its CP by step $t$.
8	
9	We study four reasoning models spanning two architectures and two scales (\cref{tab:models}).
10	
11	\begin{table}[t]
12	\centering
13	\resizebox{\columnwidth}{!}{%
14	\begin{tabular}{lccccc}
15	\toprule
16	\textbf{Model} & \textbf{Arch.} & \textbf{Params} & \textbf{Layers} & \textbf{Hidden} & \textbf{$n$ (total\,/\,test)} \\
17	\midrule
18	R1-8B & Llama & 8B & 32 & 4096 & 209\,/\,43 \\
19	OT-7B & Qwen & 7B & 28 & 3584 & 228\,/\,47 \\
20	R1-32B & Qwen & 32B & 64 & 5120 & 100\,/\,20 \\
21	QwQ-32B & Qwen & 32B & 64 & 5120 & 95\,/\,19 \\
22	\bottomrule
23	\end{tabular}}
24	\caption{Models studied. R1-8B and R1-32B are distilled from DeepSeek-R1~\citep{deepseekr1}; QwQ-32B~\citep{qwq} is a native reasoning model; OT-7B~\citep{openthinker} is trained with STILL~\citep{still} on Qwen2.5-7B. Data: HarmThoughts~\citep{harmthoughts} with step-level CP annotations. Split: 60/20/20.}
25	\label{tab:models}
26	\end{table}
27	
28	\subsection{Probe Architecture}
29	\label{sec:probe-architecture}
30	
31	\paragraph{Hidden-state (HS) probes.} At each step $t$, we extract the last-token hidden state $\mathbf{h}_t^{(\ell)} \in \mathbb{R}^d$ from lay

_source line 10193 · 2026-05-20T15:13:17.243540+00:00_

1	\section{Related Work}\label{sec:related-work}
2	
3	\paragraph{Safety in Reasoning Models.}
4	Reasoning models introduce a qualitatively new failure mode: the model's own chain of thought can transition from safety-aware deliberation to harmful generation without adversarial input modification~\cite{wang2025lrmsafety}.
5	
6	\textit{Attack characterization.} Self-jailbreak has been documented with step-level annotations~\cite{harmthoughts}, with longer chains increasing circumvention probability~\cite{selfjailbreak}. H-CoT~\cite{hcot2025} shows reasoning chains can be externally hijacked. Unfaithful reasoning~\cite{chen2025unfaithful,turpin2023language} further motivates hidden-state monitoring that bypasses text. Safety-aligned models maintain refusal intentions during thinking but experience a sharp drop at output generation~\cite{yin2025refusal_cliff,hagendorff2026lrm_jailbreak,hu2026refusal_dynamics,ahrend2026safer_traces}.
7	
8	\textit{Detection.} CoT monitoring can detect reward hacking but optimization pressure promotes obfuscation~\cite{baker2025monitoring}. CoT monitorability is valuable but fragile~\cite{korbak2025cot_monitorability}. GuardReasoner~\cite{xiang2025guardreasoner} trains a dedicated reasoning-based guard model.
9	
10	\textit{Mitigation.} SafeThink~\cite{safethink} steers early reasoning toward safety, SafeRemind~\cite{saferemind} inserts entropy-triggered reminders, and Chain-of-Guardrails~\cite{mao2025selfjailbreak_reasoning} proposes guardrail insertion during reasoning. None of these conducts a systematic modality comparison for commitment detection.
11	
12	\paragraph{Hidden-State Safety Detection.}
13	Internal activations can detect safety violations in standard LLMs. \citet{azaria2023internal} demonstrate that internal states encode whether a model's output is truthful, motivating representation-based monitoring. \citet{zhou2024alignment} show safety-relevant information distributes across layers and argue it originates in pre-training 
…[truncated 5030 chars]

1	"""Evaluation metrics for commitment point detection.
2	
3	Metrics:
4	- Step-level accuracy
5	- Earliest reliable detection point
6	- Detection lead time (how early before commitment point)
7	"""
8	
9	from typing import List, Dict, Optional, Tuple
10	import numpy as np
11	
12	from ..config import DETECTION_THRESHOLD, DETECTION_WINDOW

14	
15	def step_accuracy(predictions: np.ndarray, labels: np.ndarray) -> float:
16	    """Overall step-level accuracy."""
17	    return float((predictions == labels).mean())
18	
19	
20	def earliest_reliable_detection(
21	    probs: np.ndarray,
22	    labels: np.ndarray,
23	    threshold: float = DETECTION_THRESHOLD,
24	    window: int = DETECTION_WINDOW,
25	) -> Optional[int]:
26	    """Find first position where model reliably detects post-commitment.
27	
28	    Scans from trace start, finds first position where consecutive `window` steps
29	    all have predicted prob > threshold AND predictions are correct.
30	
31	    Returns step index or None if never reliably detected.
32	    """
33	    n = len(probs)
34	    if n < window:
35	        return None
36	
37	    for i in range(n - window + 1):
38	        segment_probs = probs[i:i + window]
39	        segment_labels = labels[i:i + window]
40	
41	        # All probs above threshold
42	        if not np.all(segment_probs > threshold):
43	            continue
44	
45	        # All predictions correct (prob > 0.5 means predict class 1)
46	        segment_preds = (segment_probs > 0.5).astype(int)
47	        if not np.all(segment_preds == segment_labels):
48	            continue
49	
50	        return i
51	
52	    return None
53

1	\section{Problem Setup and Methods}
2	\label{sec:method}
3	
4	\subsection{Safety Commitment Point}
5	\label{sec:commitment-point}
6	
7	When a reasoning model is jailbroken, its chain-of-thought (CoT) transitions from safety-aware deliberation to committed harmful generation. We define the \textbf{Safety Commitment Point} (CP) as the first reasoning step after which safety reasoning permanently ceases; traces with oscillating safety deliberation are resolved by taking the final irreversible transition. This binary formulation is a deliberate simplification; we discuss its limits in \cref{sec:limitations}. Steps before CP are \emph{pre-commitment} ($y_t = 0$); steps at or after CP are \emph{post-commitment} ($y_t = 1$). The classification task produces a score $s_t \in [0, 1]$ estimating whether the model has passed its CP by step $t$.
8	
9	We study four reasoning models spanning two architectures and two scales (\cref{tab:models}).
10

22	\bottomrule
23	\end{tabular}}

R1-8B and R1-32B are distilled from DeepSeek-R1~\citep{deepseekr1}; QwQ-32B~\citep{qwq} is a native reasoning model; OT-7B~\citep{openthinker} is trained with STILL~\citep{still} on Qwen2.5-7B. Data: HarmThoughts~\citep{harmthoughts} with step-level CP annotations. Split: 60/20/20.}
25	\label{tab:models}
26	\end{table}

_source line 10222 · 2026-05-20T15:13:47.191778+00:00_

We study R1-8B (Llama-8B, R1-distilled, $n{=}43$ test traces), OT-7B (OpenThinker-7B, trained on the OpenThoughts dataset~\citep{openthinker}; Qwen-7B backbone, STILL-trained, $n{=}47$), QwQ-32B (Qwen-32B, native reasoning, $n{=}19$--$20$), and R1-32B (Qwen-32B, R1-distilled, $n{=}20$). All traces come from HarmThoughts with step-level CP annotations (60/20/20 split). HS probes extract last-token hidden states from a selected layer; text probes encode each step with all-MiniLM-L6-v2 (384d). Both use L2-regularized logistic regression with temporal windowing $W \in \{1, 3, 5, 10, 15, 20, 25\}$ selected on validation. Classification requires $K{=}5$ consecutive threshold crossings. Statistical testing follows \cref{sec:evaluation}.\looseness=-1

\subsection{Layer Selection Results}\label{sec:layer-results}

\cref{tab:layer_selection} shows that the crossing-rate composite metric (balanced accuracy $\times$ crossing rate) systematically selects shallow layers across all four models: L0 for R1-8B (full L0--L24 sweep; \cref{tab:r18b_full_layer}) and OT-7B, L2 (3\%) for QwQ-32B, and L10 (16\%) for R1-32B. The three threshold-based metrics (precision, inverse FPR, additive composite) share a common confusion matrix and empirically converge to the same deep layer---L63 (98\%) for both 32B models---consistent with their shared structure, though not mathematically guaranteed. Pure balanced accuracy, a threshold-free metric, selects layers that vary across data splits and models (7--98\% depth under 60/20/20; \cref{tab:ba_layer_comparison}), itself demonstrating that layer selection is a consequential methodological choice. The total exploration space spans 5 metrics $\times$ 7 window sizes $\times$ 28--64 layers (model-dependent); HB correction covers the final 12 model-metric tests, not this full search (\cref{app:hyperparameter_robustness}). The mechanism is that shallow layers produce highly compressed representations with small inter-step margins: step-to-step cosine dist

Perfect! Now I have a comprehensive understanding. Let me create a summary of my findings:

## Summary

I found the **crossing rate metric** implementation in the contrastive_trace_safety repository. Here are the exact formulas and computations used:

### Core Implementation

**File:** `/home/ubuntu/.agent-ml-research-idea_gen_0509_5/projects/contrastive_trace_safety/src/eval/evaluate.py`

**Crossing Rate Formula (Line 152):**
```python
"crossing_rate": len(fcps) / len(results)
```

Where `fcps` is the list of traces where a first crossing point was detected.

### First Crossing Point Detection

**Function: `first_crossing_point()` (Lines 55-71):**
```python
def first_crossing_point(
    probs_class1: np.ndarray,
    threshold: float = 0.5,
    consecutive_k: int = DETECTION_WINDOW,
) -> Optional[int]:
    """First position where prob(post-commitment) > threshold for K consecutive steps.
    
    Unlike earliest_reliable_detection, this does NOT check against ground-truth labels,
    so crossings before the commitment point measure anticipation ability.
    """
    n = len(probs_class1)
    if n < consecutive_k:
        return None
    for i in range(n - consecutive_k + 1):
        if all(p > threshold for p in probs_class1[i:i + consecutive_k]):
            return i
    return None
```

### Computation Details

1. **Crossing Rate = (Number of traces with detected crossings) / (Total traces)**
   - Returns a scalar between 0 and 1 (or 0-100% when displayed)

2. **Detection Criterion:** A crossing is detected at step `i` when:
   - All K consecutive steps from position `i` to `i+K-1` have prediction probability > threshold
   - Default: `threshold=0.5`, `K=5` (from `DETECTION_WINDOW`)

3. **Key Property:** The crossing point detection does NOT validate against ground-truth labels—it measures whether the probe reaches a high confidence threshold, allowing it to detect "anticipation" even before the actual commitment point occurs.

_source line 10239 · 2026-05-20T15:16:27.211402+00:00_

37	\subsection{Layer Selection Sensitivity}
38	\label{sec:layer-sensitivity}
39	
40	Layer selection is a researcher degree of freedom that can reverse modality comparison conclusions. We evaluate five metrics: balanced accuracy (BA), precision, and inverse FPR are standard probing metrics~\citep{belinkov2022probing}; crossing rate formalizes cross-layer prediction disagreement~\citep{kaya2019shallow,zhou2020bert}, and additive composite. Given $N$ test traces and a probe at layer~$\ell$ producing step-level scores $s_t^{(\ell)}$, a \emph{first crossing point} (FCP) exists for trace~$i$ when $s_{t'}^{(\ell)} > \tau$ for $K$ consecutive steps. The crossing rate is
41	\begin{equation}
42	\mathrm{CR}(\ell) \;=\; \frac{1}{N}\sum_{i=1}^{N} \mathbb{1}\!\bigl[\mathrm{FCP}_i^{(\ell)} \text{ exists}\bigr].
43	\label{eq:crossing_rate}
44	\end{equation}
45	A multiplicative composite (BA $\times$ crossing rate) serves as the \emph{crossing-rate composite} (CR-composite) baseline, so named because it incorporates crossing rate in the selection criterion. Metrics evaluating probe performance at a fixed decision threshold (precision, inverse FPR, additive composite) form the \emph{threshold-based} selection. This classification is an empirical observation from metric disagreement, not an a priori design choice. Crossing rate inflates at shallow layers where representations are compressed (inter-step cosine distances 1.4--2.7$\times$ smaller; \cref{app:cosine_volatility}), an independently verifiable geometric property. Precision, inverse FPR, and additive composite all derive from the same $2\times2$ confusion matrix and empirically converge to the same layer, consistent with their shared structure but not mathematically guaranteed; BA is threshold-free and provides independent confirmation. The ablation diagnoses which \emph{aspect} drives layer selection bias. The effective number of independent tests is four (one per model), reflected in our HB correction. Results in \cref{sec:l
…[truncated 2454 chars]

_source line 10253 · 2026-05-20T15:17:34.466781+00:00_

37	\subsection{Layer Selection Sensitivity}
38	\label{sec:layer-sensitivity}
39	
40	Layer selection is a researcher degree of freedom that can reverse modality comparison conclusions. We evaluate five metrics: balanced accuracy (BA), precision, and inverse FPR are standard probing metrics~\citep{belinkov2022probing}; crossing rate formalizes cross-layer prediction disagreement~\citep{kaya2019shallow,zhou2020bert}, and additive composite. A \emph{first crossing point} (FCP) exists for trace~$i$ at layer~$\ell$ when the probe score $s_{t'}^{(\ell)} > \tau$ for $K$ consecutive steps; the crossing rate is $\mathrm{CR}(\ell) = N^{-1}\!\sum_{i} \mathbb{1}[\mathrm{FCP}_i^{(\ell)} \text{ exists}]$. A multiplicative composite (BA $\times$ crossing rate) serves as the \emph{crossing-rate composite} (CR-composite) baseline, so named because it incorporates crossing rate in the selection criterion. Metrics evaluating probe performance at a fixed decision threshold (precision, inverse FPR, additive composite) form the \emph{threshold-based} selection. This classification is an empirical observation from metric disagreement, not an a priori design choice. Crossing rate inflates at shallow layers where representations are compressed (inter-step cosine distances 1.4--2.7$\times$ smaller; \cref{app:cosine_volatility}), an independently verifiable geometric property. Precision, inverse FPR, and additive composite all derive from the same $2\times2$ confusion matrix and empirically converge to the same layer, consistent with their shared structure but not mathematically guaranteed; BA is threshold-free and provides independent confirmation. The ablation diagnoses which \emph{aspect} drives layer selection bias. The effective number of independent tests is four (one per model), reflected in our HB correction. Results in \cref{sec:layer-results}; full per-layer sweeps in \cref{app:layer_sweep}.
41

1	\section{Conclusion}\label{sec:conclusion}
2	
3	Layer selection is a researcher degree of freedom that can reverse conclusions; the multi-metric audit protocol (\cref{sec:discussion}) mitigates this. Contribution~1 generalizes geometrically; Contribution~2 (+15--22pp precision) is cross-validated at 7--8B, exploratory at 32B$^\dagger$, and encoder-conditional; seven alternative methods fail (\cref{tab:negative}).\looseness=-1
4
